In the spring, as his health declined and she was sleeping less to manage medications and appointments, family members noticed an abrupt shift: she began overcommitting at the community center, trying to launch multiple new senior art initiatives at once while neglecting her own medical follow‑ups. After several weeks of this increasingly driven activity, she \"crashed\" into a prolonged low period in which she stopped attending line dancing, let her garden go untended, and withdrew from her beloved workshops, leaving other volunteers to scramble to cover her responsibilities. The combination of visible changes in her functioning, missed church and book club meetings, and a worrying incident where she forgot to pick up her husband’s prescriptions led her daughter and primary care physician to press for a psychiatric evaluation, during which the pattern of mood episodes over the preceding four months became clear.\n- Chief Complaint: “I just don’t care about my painting or the garden anymore, and the things I used to look forward to feel like a burden.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): These days I walk past my easel and week.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): I used to enjoy planning our meals, but lately I have to remind myself to eat because food just seems like a chore, and my daughter keeps telling me my clothes are hanging looser on me than they did a couple of months ago.\n- S028 (Psychomotor_Changes): Most of the time now I shuffle around the house like I’m walking through molasses, but a few weeks back, when I was trying to start all those art projects at the center, I was buzzing around so fast—pacing, sorting papers, rearranging things—that I couldn’t sit still long enough to finish anything.\n- S029 (Fatigue): No matter how much I lie down, I feel bone-tired, like even carrying the laundry basket or helping my husband to the bathroom drains me for the whole day.\n- S031 (Diminished_Thinking): I find myself staring at the pill organizer or the calendar, knowing I’m supposed to decide something simple like which appointment to book first, and my mind just goes blank or spins so much that I can’t make up my mind and end up doing nothing.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): When I had that spell in the spring, I felt strangely revved up and touchy at the same time—one minute I was overly cheerful and talking a mile a minute about new classes, and the next I’d snap at my daughter over the tiniest suggestion that I slow down.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): Back when this all started, I was racing from one idea to the next at the community center, signing up for committees, reorganizing shelves, making calls late into the night, like I had endless energy and couldn’t bear to stop moving.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): During that busy period I honestly felt like I was the only one who could turn the whole senior program around, like I had some special gift for fixing everything and didn’t need anyone’s help or rest, even though that’s not how I usually see myself.\n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): My daughter kept telling me to slow down because when I got going about the art initiatives I’d just talk over everyone, jumping from one plan to another so fast they could hardly get a word in or follow what I was saying.\n- S038 (Racing_Thoughts): At night, especially during that high spell, my head would be so full of ideas—new classes, trips, ways to reorganize the house—that the thoughts felt like they were tripping over each other, and I couldn’t switch them off long enough to fall asleep.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): I went from quietly keeping up with my usual activities to suddenly trying to revamp the whole community center schedule, start new art groups, and even rearrange our house all at once, as if I was on some mission and couldn’t rest until everything was done.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functional_impairment: Since all this started, I’ve had to step back from most of my community center work, I’ve missed or mixed up several of my husband’s appointments and prescriptions, and my daughter has been coming over almost daily to help with things I used to manage on my own.\n- duration: Looking back, this whole stretch—from when I first got unusually wound up in the spring to feeling this low and slowed down now—has been going on for roughly the last four months.\n- depressive_episode_duration: This heavy, uninterested, worn‑out feeling has been hanging over me for about week.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): I used to enjoy planning our meals, but lately I have to remind myself to eat because food just seems like a chore, and my daughter keeps telling me my clothes This is the first time I've ever felt so completely low and drained.\n\nDoctor: Along with feeling low and losing interest, have you also been experiencing feelings of worthlessness or excessive guilt?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really experienced feelings of worthlessness or guilt. After several weeks of this increasingly driven activity, she \"crashed\" into a prolonged low period in which she stopped attending line dancing, let her garden go untended, and withdrew from her beloved workshops, leaving other volunteers to scramble to cover her responsibilities. The combination of visible changes in her functioning, missed church and book club meetings, and a worrying incident where she forgot to pick up her husband’s prescriptions led her daughter and painting or the garden anymore, and the things I used to look forward to feel like a burden.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): These days I walk past my easel and week.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): I used to enjoy planning our meals, but lately I have to remind myself to eat because food just seems like a chore, and my daughter keeps telling me my clothes are hanging looser on me than they did a couple of months ago.\n- S028 (Psychomotor_Changes): Most of the time now I shuffle around the house like I’m walking through molasses, but a few weeks back, when I was trying to start all those art projects at the center, I was buzzing around so fast—pacing, sorting papers, rearranging things—that I couldn’t sit still long enough to finish anything.\n- gift for fixing everything and didn’t need anyone’s help or rest, even though that’s not how I usually see myself.\n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): My daughter kept telling me to slow down because when I got going about the art initiatives I’d just talk over everyone, jumping from one plan to another so fast they could hardly get a word in or follow what I was saying.\n- S038 (Racing_Thoughts): At night, especially during that high spell, my head would be so full of ideas—new classes, trips, ways to reorganize the house—that the thoughts felt like they were tripping over each other, and I couldn’t switch them off long enough to fall asleep.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): I went from quietly keeping up with my usual activities to suddenly trying to revamp the whole community center schedule, start new art groups, and even rearrange our house all at once, as if I was on some mission and couldn’t rest until everything was done.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functional_impairment: Since all this started, I’ve had to step back from most of my community center work, I’ve missed or mixed up several of my husband’s appointments and prescriptions, and my daughter has been coming over almost daily to help with things I used to manage on my own.\n- duration: Looking back, this whole stretch—from when I first got unusually wound up in the spring to In the spring, as his health declined and she was sleeping less to manage medications and appointments, family members noticed an abrupt shift: she began overcommitting at the community center, trying to launch multiple new senior art initiatives at once while neglecting her own medical follow‑ups. After several weeks of this increasingly driven activity, she \"crashed\" into a prolonged low period in which she stopped attending line dancing, let her garden go untended, and withdrew from her beloved workshops, leaving other volunteers to scramble to cover her responsibilities. The combination of visible changes in her functioning, missed church and book club meetings, and a worrying incident where she forgot to pick up her husband’s prescriptions led her daughter and primary care physician to press for a psychiatric evaluation, during which the pattern of mood episodes over the preceding four months became clear.\n- Chief Complaint: “I just don’t care about my painting or the garden anymore, and the things I used to look forward to feel like a burden.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): These days I walk past my easel and the garden beds and feel nothing at all—no excitement, no urge to pick up a brush or pull a weed—so I just sit in my chair and let the hours go by, even though those used to be the best parts of my week.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): I used to enjoy planning our meals, but lately I have to remind myself to eat because food just seems like a chore, and my daughter keeps telling me my clothes are hanging looser on me than they did a couple of months ago.\n- S028 (Psychomotor_Changes): Most of the time now I shuffle around the house like I’m walking through molasses, but a few weeks back, when I was trying to start all those art projects at the center, I was buzzing around so fast—pacing, sorting papers, rearranging things—that I couldn’t sit still long enough to finish anything.\n- S029 (Fatigue): No matter how much I lie down, I feel bone-tired, like even carrying the laundry basket or helping my husband to the bathroom drains me for the whole day.\n- S031 (Diminished_Thinking): I find myself staring at the pill organizer or the calendar, knowing I’m supposed to decide something simple like which appointment to book first, and my mind just goes blank or spins so much that I can’t make up my mind and end up doing nothing.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): When I had that spell in the spring, I felt strangely revved up and touchy at the same time—one minute I was overly cheerful and talking a mile a minute about new classes, and the next I’d snap at my daughter over the tiniest suggestion that I slow down.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): Back when this all started, I was racing from one idea to the next at the community center, signing up for committees, reorganizing shelves, making calls late into the night, like I had endless energy and couldn’t bear to stop moving.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): During that busy period I honestly felt like I was the only one who could turn the whole senior program around, like I had some special gift for fixing everything and didn’t need anyone’s help or rest, even though that’s not how I usually see myself.\n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): My daughter kept telling me to slow down because when I got going about the art initiatives I’d just talk over everyone, jumping from one plan to another so fast they could hardly get a word in or follow what I was saying.\n- S038 (Racing_Thoughts): At night, especially during that high spell, my head would be so full of ideas—new classes, trips, ways to reorganize the house—that the thoughts felt like they were tripping over each other, and I couldn’t switch them off long enough to fall asleep.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): I went from quietly keeping up with my usual activities to suddenly trying to revamp the whole community center schedule, start new art groups, and even rearrange our house all at once, as if I was on some mission and couldn’t rest until everything was done.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functional_impairment: Since all this started, I’ve had to step back from most of my community center work, I’ve missed or mixed up several of my husband’s appointments and prescriptions, and my daughter has been coming over almost daily to help with things I used to manage on my own.\n- duration: Looking back, this whole stretch—from when I first got unusually wound up in the spring to feeling this low and slowed down now—has been going on for roughly the last four months.\n- depressive_episode_duration: This heavy, uninterested, worn‑out feeling has been hanging over me for about three months now, ever since I crashed after that busy spell at the community center.\n\n[Behavioral Guidelines]\n- In the very first turn, only talk about your chief complaint. Even though I've been feeling so low, tired, and completely uninterested in things lately, I've never thought about doing something like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your appetite or weight recently, such as eating much less or more than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes, I really have. Eating feels like a chore now, and I constantly have to remind myself to do it. My daughter has even noticed that my clothes are hanging much looser on me than they did a couple of months ago.\n\nDoctor: Are these feelings of being low, drained, and uninterested present nearly every day for most of the day, or do they tend to come and go?\n\nPatient: Yes, they are constant and present nearly every day, pretty much all day long. This heavy cloud has been hanging over me continuously for the past three months, ever since I crashed after that busy spell in the spring. It just doesn't seem to lift at all.\n\nDoctor: How has your sleep been over these past three months? Have you been having trouble falling or staying asleep, or perhaps sleeping much more than usual?\n\nPatient: I'm not quite sure about my actual sleep patterns over the last three months. I spend a lot of time lying down because I feel constantly bone-tired and exhausted, but I haven't really paid attention to whether I'm sleeping too much or having trouble falling asleep.\n\nDoctor: Have you or your family members noticed that you are moving, speaking, or thinking much more slowly than usual, or perhaps that you feel unusually restless and unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've noticed both of those extremes. Currently, I feel incredibly slowed down, shuffling around the house like I'm walking through molasses, and my mind often goes blank when I try to make simple decisions. But a few weeks ago during that high spell, I was extremely restless, buzzing around, pacing, and unable to sit still long enough to finish anything.\n\nDoctor: During that 'high spell' you mentioned, did you feel unusually energetic, hyperactive, or irritable, or notice you needed much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes, I felt extremely revved up and energetic, but also very touchy and quick to snap at my daughter. I felt like I had endless energy to race around doing things at the community center, and at night my mind would spin with so many ideas that I couldn't sleep. It was like I just couldn't switch my brain off."

Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry. Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns.  Subtypes: Mirror Checking or Avoidance, Excessive Camouflaging, Skin Picking, Reassurance Seeking, Comparing, Seeking Cosmetic Procedures\n[S069] Intrusive_Symptoms: Recurrent, involuntary, and intrusive distressing memories of the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others.  Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health. Subtypes: Auditory Startle, Tactile Startle, Visual Startle, Slow Recovery\n[S081] Stress_Related_Maladaptive_Response: Emotional or behavioral symptoms appearing in response to an identifiable stressor.  Subtypes: With Depressed Mood, With Anxiety, With Mixed Anxiety and Depressed Mood, With Disturbance of Conduct, With Mixed Disturbance of Emotions and Conduct, Unspecified\n[S082] Depersonalization_Derealization: Altered sense of the reality of one's self or surroundings after a traumatic event.
